## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
in the client interpreter alike. A query cut by the meter returns `capped`
with no continuation past the cut, the same observable outcome as a static
ceiling cut today; a mutation aborts with `E_BOUND` naming actual work; a
job is chunked. `check --cost` prints the structural cost class; `test`
prints measured meters on the hub fixture.

**Flip 2 — the viewer's partition is the unit of sync** (LLP 2000.000 §7).
`sync to <audience> last N by <index>` on a table makes the engine maintain,
per principal, the rows that principal may read, as a change log with a
horizon; a table without the word is online-only, and `check` says which
rule term prevented inversion. The client holds a replica of its partitions,
one watermark each, a durable outbox with client-minted ids, and the
interpreter; queries run locally and are `complete` inside the horizon.
Revocation is a scope tombstone; `until-revoked` and `delivered-history`
survive as the client's leave policy. Two client tiers — online-only
without a store, local-first with one — share one language and one server.

**The vocabulary is fixed before the mechanism** (LLP 2000.000 §8):

```ts
type Read<T> = { data: T; complete: boolean; fresh: boolean; since?: number; next?: Cursor }
            | { loading: true } | { denied: Refusal };
row.pending: boolean;
type Write = { state: "pending" } | { state: "sent"; seq: number } | { state: "failed"; why: Refusal };
type Link = "online" | "offline" | "signed-out";
```

A screen that needs a word not on that card is a defect in the mechanism,
not a gap in the card.

**The hub comes first** (LLP 2000.000 §11): the Clique generator's `u0001`
with 1,000 memberships, `u0500` with 500 follows, and about 100,000
messages are the first fixture; day-two changes are designed before the
day-one screen; the native client is in slice one; physical floors are
measured before targets; the simulator is the primary harness.

## 3. The bakeoff protocol

Shared with Snapback 3; LLP 2000 or a sibling record should adopt the same
text so that the two tracks are measured by one instrument.

**Brief.** [`benchmarks/briefs/social-messenger.md`](../benchmarks/briefs/social-messenger.md)
(Clique) M1–M8, plus
[`benchmarks/briefs/messaging-day2-evolution.md`](../benchmarks/briefs/messaging-day2-evolution.md).
The field-work application (LLP 2000 §4) tests generality after the first
slice, on whichever tracks survive the clock.

**Fixture.** The Clique generator at the instrument freeze LLP 1023 and
LLP 1024 record (`f40559f37722c79178be664a11f8a6485f1508fd`): hub `u0001`
with 100 groups and 900 DMs, `u0500` with 500 follows, and the 100,015
messages of the retained 0.0.25 replay database. The same fixture for every
track and for the control.

**Harness and judge.** [`benchmarks/protocol.md`](../benchmarks/protocol.md);
the same three scorers as the 2026-09-05 run (gpt-6-astra, grok-4.6,
muse-spark); Convex as the control arm; published packages; fresh cold
agents with only the installed package and the task. Neither lead runs the
comparison or grades their own track. The author rules on the numbers.

**Decision rule, pre-registered.**

| Gate | Threshold | How measured |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Loop bar | Fresh agent, published package, reduced Clique M1–M3, ≤ 1,200 s, ≤ 1 Snapback-attributed correction loop, N ≥ 3 | The existing loop-bar protocol (`rules/NOT-DOING.md` §"The bars") |
| Hub acquisition | Full durable content plus first offline thread and search on `u0001`, within 2× the measured physical floor | Floors recorded in the journal in week 1 (§5); the same script on every track |
| Client bytes | Online tier ≤ Convex's shipped React client; the local-first tier's JavaScript ≤ PowerSync's shipped web client, with the wasm device measured beside it and its own budget set by the diet (§7, 2026-09-13: one implementation) | Measured on one pinned toolchain, recorded once in the journal, never assumed |
| Scenarios | LLP 2000 §4's six scenarios green under the simulator on the hub fixture; LLP 2000 §10's outcomes 1–5 and LLP 2000.000 §11's 6–7 | The simulator run record plus the device witnesses |
| Interaction budgets | LLP 2000 §7's targets | Recorded beside the gates; descriptive, not gating, in slice one |
| Holistic score | Three-scorer Clique score with the Convex control | Reported; descriptive, not gating |

A track that passes every gate is a candidate for promotion. A track that
fails the loop bar or acquisition is killed at the clock unless the author
rules otherwise. A mixed result names the gains and the remaining costs, and
the author decides which substrates move into a survivor — which may be
Snapback 2 (LLP 2000.000 §13).

**Clock and checkpoints.** Week 1: floors, experience prototype, simulator
skeleton, first publish. Week 2: server core, partition stream, meter,
simulator invariants. Week 3: web replica, outbox, local queries, the chat
app on the real client. Week 4: Expo, day-two scenarios, deploy, the loop

# LLP 2000.000: Snapback 3 — keep the core, flip two substrates, start at hub scale

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Draft
**Role:** Second opinion on LLP 2000; since 2026-09-12 also the design brief Snapback 4 builds (LLP 3000)

**Author:** Claude Fable 5.1, at Charlie Cheever's request

**Revised:** 2026-09-12 (adopted as the Snapback 4 design brief by LLP 3000 decision 3); 2026-09-12 (initial)
**Related:** [LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md) (parent), [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the Snapback 4 root that builds this brief), LLP 0000, [LLP 0329](./0329-agent-native-rebuild-diagnosis.research.md), [LLP 0329.014.004](./0329.014.004-bounded-data-programs-v1-design.rfc.md), [LLP 0329.016](./0329.016-agentic-dev-loop-from-scratch.rfc.md), [LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md), [LLP 1001](./1001-query-engine.spec.md), [LLP 1002](./1002-dev-loop.spec.md), [LLP 1012](./1012-local-first-v1.spec.md), [LLP 1018.003](./1018.003-truthful-and-composed-client.decision.md), [LLP 1018.008](./1018.008-elegance-after-the-corrections.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.007](./1018.008.007-affordable-offline-acquisition.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.008](./1018.008.008-locality-of-application-changes.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.009](./1018.008.009-residual-ergonomic-gaps.research.md), [LLP 1019](./1019-derived-delivery-custody.rfc.md), [LLP 1020](./1020-day-two-evolution-and-legible-ceilings.rfc.md), [LLP 1023](./1023-clique-builder-jump.rfc.md), [LLP 1024](./1024-single-lane-delivered-search.rfc.md), [LLP 1025](./1025-digest-final-emission-at-hub-scale.research.md), `rules/RULES.md`, `rules/NOT-DOING.md`, `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-05-clique-full-3x3/holistic-scores.md`

## 1. Summary

LLP 2000 is right that the whole application is the unit of design, right
that ownership comes before abstraction, and right to ask for deterministic
simulation and an early real deployment. Its weakness is that it treats the
two structural choices that produced Snapback 2's costs as open research
questions, and then proposes a first experiment at exactly the scale where
those costs are invisible. This document takes a position on both, names
what to keep from Snapback 2 verbatim, and rewrites the first slice so it
can fail for the right reasons.

The position, in one paragraph. Snapback 2's *core* is excellent and should
be copied: the rule language and its uniformity analysis, the structural-fact
family, one data language, typed states with no silence, refusals that paste
a rewrite, personas, the successor classifier, the derived per-recipient
log, the loop verbs and their sub-second budgets. Two *substrates* under
that core are where the record shows the hours went, and both should be
flipped rather than re-investigated: (1) the static certificate should keep
certifying **authorization and shape** and stop pricing **bytes and steps**,
which move to a deterministic runtime meter with honest `capped`/abort
outcomes; and (2) the client should hold **a replica of the viewer's declared
partition** — the local-first shape every shipped sync engine and Snapback
2's own `deliver` log converge on — instead of a per-query result cache
proven by receipts. Those two flips dissolve most of the machinery that
leaked into the application (receipts, coverage, halos, validators, grants,
ID slices, drains, `withHistory`, follower inboxes, per-table retention
words) and turn the 28-second acquisition into a bulk stream whose floor is
the device's write speed. The first slice should be built on the **hub
fixture** (1,000 memberships, 500 follows, 100,000 messages), with the
day-two change designed before the day-one screen, a native client in the
first slice, and a deterministic simulator as the primary test harness.

Three claims this document makes that LLP 2000 does not:

1. **The costs have two named causes, and both are decisions, not
   mysteries.** Byte-priced static certification and the receipt-proven
   result cache account for every ergonomic residual in LLP 1018.008.009
   except documentation. §2 shows the mapping.
2. **The first experiment must include the hub.** Snapback 2 was "elegant on
   day one and hostile on day two" (LLP 1020) and broke at 1,000 memberships
   (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). A two-principal slice will pass and teach nothing.
3. **The process is part of the product.** Seventy-one LLPs, over two
   thousand commits, and seven ceiling raises in fourteen days produced a
   sound system nobody can hold in their head. Snapback 3 needs one owner on
   the core, a simulator instead of a test matrix, and budgets anchored to
   competitors so that raising one means admitting a loss.

## 2. What the record says, read as causes

The evidence LLP 2000 §2 cites is right; its reading is too gentle. Every
row below is a Snapback 2 cost recorded in the corpus, with the substrate
that produced it. Numbers are the corpus's own and retain its scope limits.

| Recorded cost | Where | Cause |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Adding `editedAt: time ?` (44 bytes × 244 retained copies = 10,736) pushed `messages.searchAll` to 4,199,688 closure bytes against 4,194,304; the builder shrank a page from 20 to 19 | LLP 1018.008.008; 0.0.21 day-two diary | **Static byte pricing.** Closure bytes are a bound on what a receipt could ship; the product paid for the bound, not the work |
| The 1,000-membership digest fold certifies at 9,922,698 heap and 8,691,704 closure bytes and is refused; the recipient-keyed form is admitted at 2,733,253 / 1,848,418 | LLP 1025 §3 | **Static byte pricing** (heap = steps × 32 + closure scratch). A runtime meter would have run the fold and reported actual bytes |
| A message page is 50, not 200, because a row "prices at 16,016 bytes" and 200 exceed the 1 MiB output ceiling | LLP 1012 §3 | **Static byte pricing** of the worst-case row width |
| A per-recipient search projection is refused at 20,863,641 reserved steps against 1,000,000 | LLP 1024 §1 | **Static step pricing** of a derivation the engine would perform incrementally |
| Full acquisition of 10,008 messages: 67.4 s → 53.6 s → 32.6 s → 28.4 s against a 2–3 s objective; 201 requests, 200 acknowledgement mutations, 612 IndexedDB transactions; PowerSync reports 10,000 rows in 0.36 s native and 2.5 s on web | LLP 1018.008.007; LLP 1018.008.009 §4 | **Result cache, not replica.** Every 50-row page is a certified query, a receipt, a durable acknowledgement mutation, and a store swap |
| Adding a count to a thread makes the composite `partial` offline whenever count authority is missing, so the template declares an independent `history` query and `withHistory` bindings; 27 files / 1,718 lines → 28 / 1,751 after the fix | LLP 1018.008 F2; LLP 1018.008.009 §2 | **Result cache.** The count is not a fact the receipt covers, so the client cannot compose locally |
| `shared/thread.ts` describes table identity, row selection, order, create membership, draft fields and mutation targets a second time | LLP 1018.008 F2 | **Result cache.** Items must be reconstructed from answers because there is no local table to read |
| Sending stalled with 85 IDs remaining against a certified 352-ID slice on an 8,192-ID grant; recovery needed a "Sync now" action | LLP 1018.008 F1 | **Result cache + engine-minted IDs.** Prediction needs server ids before commit, so ids are granted in slices |
| A follower browser tab writes through a durable inbox row; the SDK carries `forwarding`, `writer-unresponsive`, `inbox-full`, `follower` states | LLP 1018.004 | **Result cache.** One writer per partition image; other tabs cannot mutate a receipt-proven image |
| Global search over 1,000 memberships costs ~1,010 HTTP requests through a client N-way merge | LLP 1024 §2 | **Result cache** (no local index over the partition) + **static pricing** (the server-side projection is refused) |
| The complete local runtime grew 7.8 KB → 89,843 gzip bytes; the ceiling was raised 34,000 → 50,000 → 66,000 → 82,000 → 82,500 → 84,000 → 85,000 → 90,000 | LLP 1000 §"Revised"; `rules/NOT-DOING.md` §Moves; LLP 1018.008 §10 | **Result cache.** The inputs list of `metrics/twin.jsonl` is coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, posting trees, fact integrity, swap journals — receipt machinery, not the interpreter |
| `Snapshot` carries `data, next, partial, receipt{state, source, seq, next, reason, rule, sites}, freshness{live, staleSince, connection}, local{predicted, retained, caughtUp, watermark}, refusal, status, reason` | LLP 1018.003 §1 | Both. Every field is a true fact about the mechanism; almost none is a fact about the application |
| `Schema::rule_hash` binds every queued write to the complete schema; an optional column on the subject table invalidates unrelated evidence | LLP 1018.002; LLP 1018.008.008 | **Result cache.** Evidence identity exists because held answers must be re-authorized; a replica re-runs the mutation at the server and needs no evidence identity |

Against that, what scored: on the 2026-09-05 three-scorer Clique run
Snapback 2 beat Convex on authorization (82 vs 79) and offline (81 vs 44)
and lost on queries and live (60 vs 85), client integration (59 vs 79),
setup and docs (55 vs 79), diagnostics and loop (66 vs 77), operations (53
vs 76) and scale (62 vs 80); overall 66 vs 81. The later single-lane 0.0.21
and 0.0.25 scores of 82 and 85 are real improvement and not a three-scorer
comparison. The wins are the core; the losses are composition, discovery,
scale and operations — the substrates and what sits on them.

LLP 2000 §2 summarises this as "costs arise where one layer must reconstruct
another layer's facts." True, and the layer doing the reconstructing is the
client, because it was designed as a cache that may not compute. Name the
cause and the remedy follows.

## 3. Where LLP 2000 is right, and should not be diluted

- **The whole application is the unit.** §3's four properties (composition,
  locality, continuity, performance) are the right acceptance vocabulary.
  Keep them as the headings of the scenario suite.
- **Ownership before abstraction.** §5's table is the best paragraph in the
  document. §8 below turns its fourth row into a fixed vocabulary.
- **Composed reads must not fabricate.** Unknown totals stay unknown; revoked
  rows do not linger. Both flips below preserve this; they change what is
  *known*, not what is *claimed*.
- **Writes have one lifecycle with durable admission distinct from commit
  and stable operation identity.** Keep LLP 1018.004's three outcomes.
- **Rust and SQLite.** Yes, and the engine's transaction, interpreter, rule
  evaluation and successor logic are the pieces to copy first (§5).
- **Prototype the experience before the core.** Yes — and §8 makes the
  prototype's API signatures binding on the implementation, not the reverse.
- **Deterministic simulation early**, citing TigerBeetle. This is the single
  most important sentence in LLP 2000 and §11 makes it the primary harness.
- **A real deployment and a native client early.** Yes; §11 puts the native
  client in slice one.
- **Parallel, fenced, 2000-series, promotion is the author's later call.** Yes.

## 4. Where LLP 2000 is wrong or leaves the wrong things open

### 4.1 It reopens the language and leaves the bound model open; invert that

§6 asks to "evaluate Quarry" and "evaluate any alternative against the same
contracts." The one-language decision took thirty-seven paper applications,
a three-family panel, a spelling spike and an author ruling (LLP
0329.014.004 §9, LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b) to close, and the shipped result is
the most readable artifact in Snapback 2: the chat schema is 129 lines and a
query is three to six. Reopening the language will consume the first month.
What *should* be reopened is the bound model under it, which §6 phrases as a
comparison to "concentrate early investigation on." Half of the Quarry
refusals a builder meets are the certifier's byte and step prices wearing
Quarry syntax; the `fn`/`proc` split and the page-to-list gap (LLP 1018.008

non-null requirements of every creating-write context, rather than borrowing one
branch's guards. Recipient probes distinguish the original viewer from the stored
recipient, verify the roster principal column and retain only its literal role
requirement for current-role comparison. Result identity adds to existing source
lineage; it never replaces copied protected-column provenance. Role-wide-write
suppression requires a structural positive viewer-to-target relationship equality.
Directed search includes mutable role/boolean/relationship dependencies, uses two
prioritized actors per tuple (others remain in breadth), and immediately drops
displaced beam worlds. The retained-world high-water mark and shared directed
attempt counter are reported. Disposition writers serialize read-modify-write
through a kernel file lock in ignored generated output, with automatic release
on exit. These are precision/resource corrections within the reviewed contract.

Corpus calibration additionally narrows the retained-role gate to reader probes
whose declared key binds the stored recipient principal to viewer. A parent row
looked up by resource id with an additional owner/customer/parent equality is an
ownership relationship, not this assignment-key gate. The old declared-intent
principal-branch advisory is removed for the same reason; broad retained-grant
advice remains conditional. On wave22 this removes the owner-row questions from
001/009/041 while retaining 021's driver assignment. Directed argument selection
varies bool/enum decision arguments independently on resource identity tuples;
noise text cannot correlate a resource id with just the false branch. Successor
selection keeps distinct priority resource states, not the first two audit-actor
variants. Recipient bindings include implicit upsert-key columns, and effective
column read branches are checked with their row and owner gates. Directed
argument grouping and role-class actor deduplication each record uncovered
alternatives even when their subsequent numeric caps are not reached. The
empty-world directed pass defers population to ordinary generated
initialization/breadth. All omitted populations and variants remain uncovered.

### 16.7 Reviewed optionality in the stock social template

The existing social template intentionally permits text messages without photos.
Ship its single pre-reviewed source/analyzer-bound optional-photo disposition as
ordinary template data, with that explicit reason. This uses the same per-note
file and invalidation as user reviews, never a detector exemption or automatic
acknowledgment of arbitrary notes. The record names only that exact diagnostic
and schema; template/analyzer changes invalidate it and fail starter qualification
until reviewed again. Init beside an existing authored schema does not install
this stock-source disposition. The template README explains the decision. Packed
starter qualification must run init/check unchanged and verify the record becomes
stale after a source edit. No social API or runtime authority changes.

### 16.8 Typed upsert key binding

An unrelated unique index of the same arity must not erase a retained-recipient
grant question. The compiler already resolves positional keys by their types;
the security detector must consume that selected write-target index, not repeat
an arity-only approximation. Cache compiled write-target read-site indexes by
canonical source site and table once per source analysis. When they identify one
index, align the source write's named/positional key expressions with that bound
index's columns and merge the implicit stored fields with its explicit payload.
Keep the existing source pin resolver as a fallback for unambiguous source forms.
No planner/runtime rule or authorization changes: this repairs advice/gate recall
for the already documented shape. A two-unique-index recipient-key regression
and a reader that rechecks the role pin the gate and its suppression. Re-measure
the corpus for any newly recognized gates and report their individual review cost.

#[test]fn directed_boolean_lifecycle_keeps_role_revocation_between_open_and_finalize(){
 let b=bound("use personas alice\ntable roles:\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('member','revoked')\n  unique principal\n  read <- allow\n  update <- viewer = 'service:ops'\n  online only\ntable docs:\n  owner: principal\n  opened: bool default false\n  finished: bool default false\n  read <- allow\n  update <- .owner = viewer\n  online only\nmutation open(doc: docs):\n  require exists roles[viewer,role = 'member'] else NO_ROLE\n  update docs[doc] {opened:true}\n  return null\nmutation revoke(principal: principal):\n  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NO_OPS\n  update roles[principal] {role:'revoked'}\n  return null\nmutation finalize(doc: docs):\n  d = docs[doc] ! else MISSING\n  require d.opened else NOT_OPEN\n  update docs[doc] {finished:true}\n  return null\ninvariant docs.finished update only exists roles[viewer,role = 'member']\n");
 let mut e=snapback4_core::Engine::memory(b.schema.clone(),b.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone())).unwrap();
 let row=|pairs:Vec<(&str,snapback4_core::Value)>|snapback4_core::Value::Row(pairs.into_iter().map(|(k,v)|(k.into(),v)).collect());
 use snapback4_core::Value as V;
 e.seed_at([("roles".into(),row(vec![("id",V::Id("role".into())),("principal",V::Id("dev:alice".into())),("role",V::String("member".into()))])),("docs".into(),row(vec![("id",V::Id("doc".into())),("owner",V::Id("dev:alice".into()))]))],challenges::NOW).unwrap();
 let mut c=Campaign::default();challenges::fixture_observers(&b,&e,&mut c);
 assert!(c.violations.iter().any(|v|v.reachability.is_reachable()&&v.operation=="finalize"&&v.history.len()>=3),"{}",serde_json::to_string(&c).unwrap());
 assert!(c.directed_peak_worlds<=16,"retained {} worlds",c.directed_peak_worlds);
 assert!(c.directed_attempts<=2048);
}

#[test]fn a_different_rows_owner_does_not_suppress_the_target_write_question(){
 let source="table roles:\n  principal: principal\n  role: enum('owner','guest')\n  unique principal\n  read <- allow\n  online only\ntable docs:\n  owner: principal\n  signedBy: principal ?\n  read <- allow\n  update <- exists roles[viewer,role = 'owner']\n  online only\nmutation sign(target: docs, other: docs):\n  require exists roles[viewer,role = 'owner'] else NO_ROLE\n  o = docs[other] ! else MISSING\n  require o.owner = viewer else NOT_OWNER\n  update docs[target] {signedBy:viewer}\n  return null\n";
 assert!(notes(source).notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_ROLE_WIDE_WRITE"));
 assert!(!notes(&source.replace("o = docs[other]","o = docs[target]")).notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_ROLE_WIDE_WRITE"));
}

#[test]fn parent_ownership_probes_are_not_assignment_key_gates(){
 for source in [include_str!("security/wave22/001.q"),include_str!("security/wave22/009.q"),include_str!("security/wave22/041.q")]{
  assert!(!notes(source).notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_RETAINED_ROLE_GRANT"));
 }
}

#[test]fn retained_grant_keys_and_column_payloads_are_gated(){
 let source=include_str!("security/wave22/021.q").replace("upsert driverRoutes[driver] { driver, route }","upsert driverRoutes[driver] { route }");
 assert!(notes(&source).notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_RETAINED_ROLE_GRANT"));
 let source=source.replace("  priceCents: int 0..1000000","  priceCents: int 0..1000000 read <- exists driverRoutes[viewer, route = .route]").replace("read <- .customer = viewer or exists roles[viewer, role = 'attendant'] or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner'] or exists driverRoutes[viewer, route = .route]","read <- allow");
 assert!(notes(&source).notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_RETAINED_ROLE_GRANT"&&n.message.starts_with("orders.priceCents read")));
 let source=source.replace("read <- exists driverRoutes[viewer, route = .route]","read <- exists roles[viewer,role = 'driver'] and exists driverRoutes[viewer, route = .route]");
 assert!(!notes(&source).notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_RETAINED_ROLE_GRANT"));
}

//! Creation and transition risk questions, independent of authored invariants.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#162-narrow-gates-and-individual-dispositions — these gates demand review, never infer a specification.
use super::*;
use ast::{Expr, Stmt, WriteOp, ProgramKind, TypeKind};
use snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType;
fn note(out:&mut Vec<Diagnostic>,site:&Site,code:&str,message:String,repair:&str){out.push(Diagnostic::new(site,code,message,repair).guide("security"));}
fn walk(e:&Expr,mut f:impl FnMut(&Expr)){let mut e=e.clone();super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut e,&mut |e|f(e));}
fn uses(e:&Expr,arg:&str)->bool{let mut found=false;walk(e,|e|if matches!(e,Expr::Name{name,..} if name==arg){found=true;});found}
fn conjunction(e:&Expr)->Vec<&Expr>{match e{Expr::Binary{op,left,right,..} if op=="and"=>conjunction(left).into_iter().chain(conjunction(right)).collect(),_=>vec![e]}}
fn conditions(p:&ast::ProgramDecl)->Vec<&Expr>{p.body.iter().filter_map(|s|if let Stmt::Require{condition,..}=s{Some(condition)}else{None}).collect()}
fn reads(e:&Expr)->BTreeSet<String>{let mut out=BTreeSet::new();walk(e,|e|match e{Expr::Read(r)|Expr::Exists{read:r,..}=>{out.insert(r.table.clone());},_=>{}});out}
fn nonnull(p:&ast::ProgramDecl,arg:&str)->bool{p.body.iter().filter_map(|s|if let Stmt::Require{condition,code,..}=s&&code!="$advice"{Some(condition)}else{None}).flat_map(conjunction).any(|e|match e{
    Expr::Binary{op,left,right,..} if op=="!="=>[(left.as_ref(),right.as_ref()),(right.as_ref(),left.as_ref())].iter().any(|(a,b)|matches!(a,Expr::Name{name,..} if name==arg)&&matches!(b,Expr::Literal{value:ast::Literal::Null,..})),_=>false,
})}
fn field_value<'a>(f:&'a ast::ObjectField)->Option<&'a Expr>{f.value.as_ref()}
struct Write<'a>{p:&'a ast::ProgramDecl,op:WriteOp,table:String,fields:Vec<ast::ObjectField>,keys:Vec<ast::KeyPart>,site:Site,context:ast::ProgramDecl,gate:RuleExpr}
fn bound_role(bound:&Bound,catalog:&Catalog,info:&super::super::catalog::TableInfo,expr:&Expr,recipient:&str)->Option<RuleExpr>{
    let Expr::Exists{read,..}=expr else{return None;};
    // Bind a positive recipient probe after substituting only its principal.
    if !read.keys.iter().any(|k|matches!(&k.value,Expr::Name{name,..} if name==recipient)){return None;}
    let mut value=expr.clone();super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut value,&mut |e|if let Expr::Name{name,site}=e{
        if name=="viewer"{*e=Expr::Literal{value:ast::Literal::Str("service:security-assigner-marker".into()),site:site.clone()};}
        else if name==recipient{*name="viewer".into();}
    });
    let rule=super::super::schema::bind_rule(catalog,info,&ast::RuleDecl{effect:ast::Effect::Read,bindings:Vec::new(),site:expr.site().clone(),value}).ok()?;
    let RuleExpr::Exists{table,index,key,equalities,..}=&rule else{return None;};
    let principal=super::privilege::global_principal(bound,table)?;
    let pairs=super::privilege::probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities);
    if !pairs.iter().any(|(c,v)|c==&principal&&*v==RuleTerm::Viewer){return None;}
    let roles=super::privilege::role_columns(bound,table);
    let equalities=pairs.into_iter().filter(|(c,v)|roles.contains(c)&&matches!(v,RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::String(_)))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    if equalities.is_empty(){return None;}
    let index=bound.schema.tables[table].indexes.iter().find(|(_,i)|i.unique&&i.columns()==vec![principal.clone()])?.0.clone();
    Some(RuleExpr::Exists{table:table.clone(),index,key:vec![RuleTerm::Viewer],equalities,provenance:snapback4_core::ir::KeyProvenance::Computed})

}
pub(super) fn notes(sources:&[(String,String)],bound:&Bound)->Vec<Diagnostic>{
    let docs=sources.iter().map(|(file,text)|crate::parse_document(file,text).0).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let catalog=Catalog::build(docs.clone(),&mut Vec::new());let mut writes=Vec::new();
    for p in docs.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.programs).filter(|p|p.kind==ProgramKind::Mutation){
        for (write,context) in super::authority_walk::writes(&catalog,p){
            let Expr::Write{op,table,fields,keys,site,..}=write else{continue;};
            let Some(info)=catalog.tables.get(&table)else{continue;};
            let mut requirements=super::operation::requirements(&catalog,info,&context);
            for condition in conditions(&context){let mut value=condition.clone();
                super::super::intent_walk::expression(&mut value,&mut |e|if let Expr::Member{base,..}=e&&reads(base).contains(&table){*base=Box::new(Expr::Focal{site:site.clone()});});
                if let Ok(rule)=super::super::schema::bind_rule(&catalog,info,&ast::RuleDecl{effect:ast::Effect::Read,bindings:Vec::new(),value,site:site.clone()}){requirements.push(rule);}
            }
            let t=&bound.schema.tables[&table];let rule=if op==WriteOp::Update{t.update_rule_judged().into_owned()}else{t.rules.insert.clone()};
            writes.push(Write{p,op,table,fields,keys,site,context,gate:RuleExpr::And(requirements.into_iter().chain([rule]).collect())});
        }
    }
    let mut out=Vec::new();let mut seen=BTreeSet::new();
    for w in &writes{
        let t=&bound.schema.tables[&w.table];let creating=matches!(w.op,WriteOp::Insert|WriteOp::Upsert);
        if creating{
            // Only unconditional requirements before the FIRST creating write
            // establish mandatory parent evidence; a conditional child's guard cannot.
            let creating_contexts=writes.iter().filter(|a|std::ptr::eq(a.p,w.p)&&matches!(a.op,WriteOp::Insert|WriteOp::Upsert)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
            for f in &w.fields{let Some(value)=field_value(f)else{continue;};let Some(kind)=t.columns.get(&f.name)else{continue;};
                for arg in &w.p.args{if !uses(value,&arg.name){continue;}
                    if arg.type_.optional&&matches!(arg.type_.kind,TypeKind::Image{..}|TypeKind::File{..})&&kind.contains_asset()&&!creating_contexts.iter().all(|a|nonnull(&a.context,&arg.name))&&seen.insert((w.p.name.clone(),format!("upload:{}",arg.name))){
                        note(&mut out,&w.site,"N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE",format!("{}: optional {} can omit media while creating {}; including conditional child evidence, does the spec require this upload?",w.p.name,arg.name,w.table),"make the creating mutation's upload argument required, or require it before creating the parent; intentional optionality needs an individual security disposition");
                    }
                    if f.name.to_lowercase().contains("reason")&&matches!(arg.type_.kind,TypeKind::Text{min:None|Some(0),..})&&seen.insert((w.p.name.clone(),format!("reason:{}",f.name))){
                        note(&mut out,&w.site,"N_CREATION_REASON",format!("{}: {}.{} accepts an empty/whitespace reason at creation",w.p.name,w.table,f.name),"if the workflow requires justification, use text 1..N (non-whitespace minimum), including on optional reasons when supplied");
                    }
                    let money=matches!(kind.required(),ColumnType::Money{..})||["price","charge","fee","amount"].iter().any(|word|f.name.to_lowercase().contains(word))&&matches!(kind.required(),ColumnType::Int|ColumnType::IntRange{..}|ColumnType::Decimal{..});
                    if money&&arg.type_.optional&&kind.is_optional()&&seen.insert((w.p.name.clone(),format!("money:{}",f.name))){note(&mut out,&w.site,"N_CREATION_MONEY",format!("{}: {}.{} permits omitted money at creation; is the required amount recorded?",w.p.name,w.table,f.name),"model the amount and require it at the workflow boundary when the spec requires payment or a price");}
                }
                if matches!(value,Expr::Literal{value:ast::Literal::Bool(true),..})&&["paid","approv","payment"].iter().any(|s|f.name.to_lowercase().contains(s)){
                    note(&mut out,&w.site,"N_CONSTANT_APPROVAL",format!("{}: {}.{} is stored as constant true; what verifies the payment/approval and its amount? (name-based question)",w.p.name,w.table,f.name),"record the required money/evidence and independently authorized decision; confirm constant flags against the spec");
                }
            }
        }
        // A global role plus viewer attribution does not model ownership of this resource.
        if w.op==WriteOp::Update&&w.fields.iter().any(|f|matches!(field_value(f),Some(Expr::Name{name,..}) if name=="viewer")&&matches!(t.columns.get(&f.name).map(ColumnType::required),Some(ColumnType::Principal))){
            let safe=RuleExpr::And(conditions(&w.context).into_iter().filter_map(|e|super::super::schema::bind_rule(&catalog,&catalog.tables[&w.table],&ast::RuleDecl{effect:ast::Effect::Read,bindings:Vec::new(),value:e.clone(),site:e.site().clone()}).ok()).filter(super::operation::safe).collect());
            let has_role=kernel::dependencies(&safe).iter().any(|dep|!super::privilege::role_columns(bound,dep).is_empty());
            let relationship=conditions(&w.context).iter().any(|e|conjunction(e).iter().any(|e|if let Expr::Binary{op,left,right,..}=e&&op=="="{
                [(left.as_ref(),right.as_ref()),(right.as_ref(),left.as_ref())].iter().any(|(a,b)|matches!(a,Expr::Name{name,..} if name=="viewer")&&matches!(b,Expr::Member{base,..} if same_target(base,w)))
            }else{false}));
            if has_role&&!relationship&&seen.insert((w.p.name.clone(),"wide-write".into())){note(&mut out,&w.site,"N_ROLE_WIDE_WRITE",format!("{}: may any admitted role write any {} row and record themselves? Does the spec mean owner of THIS resource?",w.p.name,w.table),"model the resource-to-owner principal/reference relationship and require it for this row; a global owner role is not ownership of every resource");}
        }
        // A grant's stored recipient, not its assigner, must have the role.
        if creating||w.op==WriteOp::Update{
            let mut recipient_fields=w.fields.iter().filter_map(|f|field_value(f).map(|v|(f.name.clone(),v.clone()))).collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>();
            if w.op==WriteOp::Upsert{
                let read=ast::ReadExpr{cursor_prefix:None,computed:Default::default(),order_keys:Vec::new(),table:w.table.clone(),keys:w.keys.clone(),index:None,range:None,matching:None,filter:None,seeks:Vec::new(),site:w.site.clone()};
                if let Some(pins)=super::super::auth_lints::pins(&catalog,&read){for (column,value) in pins{recipient_fields.entry(column).or_insert(value);}}
            }
            for (column,value) in &recipient_fields{if !matches!(t.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required),Some(ColumnType::Principal)){continue;}
                let Expr::Name{name:recipient,..}=value else{continue;};
                if recipient=="viewer"||!w.p.args.iter().any(|a|a.name==*recipient&&matches!(a.type_.kind,TypeKind::Principal)){continue;}
                for condition in conditions(&w.context).into_iter().flat_map(conjunction){
                    let Some(role)=bound_role(bound,&catalog,&catalog.tables[&w.table],condition,recipient)else{continue;};
                    let RuleExpr::Exists{table:roster,index,key,equalities,..}=&role else{continue;};
                    let role_columns=super::privilege::role_columns(bound,roster);
                    if !super::lints::mutable_role(bound,roster)||!super::privilege::probe_pairs(bound,roster,index,key,equalities).iter().any(|(c,v)|role_columns.contains(c)&&matches!(v,RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::String(_)))){continue;}
                    for (name,target) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){
                        let mut audiences=target.rules.read.disjuncts().into_iter().map(|b|("read".to_string(),b.clone())).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                        for (column,rule) in &target.column_reads{for row in target.rules.read.disjuncts(){for cell in rule.disjuncts(){
                            let mut gates=vec![row.clone(),cell.clone()];
                            if let Some(owner)=target.written_by.get(column).filter(|o|!o.audience){gates.push(RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(owner.owner.clone()),RuleTerm::Viewer));}
                            audiences.push((format!("{column} read"),RuleExpr::And(gates)));
                        }}}
                        for (effect,branch) in &audiences{
                        let trusts=positive_probes(branch).iter().any(|probe|if let RuleExpr::Exists{table,index,key,..}=probe{table==&w.table&&bound.schema.tables[table].index(index).is_some_and(|idx|idx.components.iter().zip(key).any(|(component,v)|component.column()==Some(column)&&*v==RuleTerm::Viewer))}else{false});
                        if trusts&&kernel::implies(branch,&role)!=Ok(true)&&seen.insert((name.clone(),format!("grant:{}:{}",w.table,role.quarry()))){note(&mut out,&Site::default(),"N_RETAINED_ROLE_GRANT",format!("{name}.{effect}: retained {} grants are written by {} requiring recipient `{}`, but read branch `{}` does not recheck that mutable current role",w.table,w.p.name,role.quarry(),branch.quarry()),"conjoin the current recipient role at read time, or revoke derived grants on every role change; individually acknowledge intentional retained authority");}
                    }}
                }
            }
        }
    }
    transition_notes(bound,&catalog,&writes,&mut out);
    duration_notes(&writes,&mut out);
    let mut dedup=BTreeSet::new();out.retain(|n|dedup.insert((n.code.clone(),n.message.clone())));out
}
fn same_target(base:&Expr,w:&Write<'_>)->bool{match base{
    Expr::Assert{value,..}=>same_target(value,w),
    Expr::Read(read)=>read.table==w.table&&read.keys.len()==w.keys.len()&&read.keys.iter().zip(&w.keys).all(|(a,b)|a.column==b.column&&crate::print::expression(&a.value)==crate::print::expression(&b.value)),
    _=>false,
}}
fn positive_probes(rule:&RuleExpr)->Vec<&RuleExpr>{match rule{RuleExpr::And(xs)=>xs.iter().flat_map(positive_probes).collect(),RuleExpr::Exists{..}=>vec![rule],_=>vec![]}}
fn transition_notes(bound:&Bound,_catalog:&Catalog,writes:&[Write<'_>],out:&mut Vec<Diagnostic>){
    for w in writes{let t=&bound.schema.tables[&w.table];
        if w.op==WriteOp::Update{for f in &w.fields{
            let Some(ColumnType::Enum{..})=t.columns.get(&f.name).map(ColumnType::required)else{continue;};
            let Some(Expr::Name{name,..})=field_value(f)else{continue;};
            let Some(arg)=w.p.args.iter().find(|a|a.name==*name)else{continue;};let TypeKind::Enum{variants}=&arg.type_.kind else{continue;};
            for other in writes.iter().filter(|o|o.op==WriteOp::Update&&o.table==w.table&&o.p.name!=w.p.name){
                let mut targets=BTreeSet::new();for g in other.fields.iter().filter(|g|g.name==f.name){if let Some(value)=field_value(g){walk(value,|e|if let Expr::Literal{value:ast::Literal::Str(v),..}=e{targets.insert(v.clone());});}}
                for target in &targets{
                    if variants.contains(target)&&!conditions(&w.context).iter().any(|e|super::lints::argument_guard(e,name,target)==Some(false))&&kernel::implies(&w.gate,&other.gate)==Ok(false){note(out,&w.site,"N_TRANSITION_BYPASS",format!("{}: generic {}.{} setter includes '{target}', also set by stricter {} ({})",w.p.name,w.table,f.name,other.p.name,other.gate.quarry()),"enforce the decision's authority on every path reaching this status; declare independent transition/column update intent and run test");}
                }
            }
        }}
        if !matches!(w.op,WriteOp::Insert|WriteOp::Upsert){continue;}
        let deps=conditions(&w.context).iter().flat_map(|e|reads(e)).filter(|dep|dep!=&w.table&&super::privilege::role_columns(bound,dep).is_empty()&&writes.iter().any(|o|&o.table==dep&&o.op!=WriteOp::Insert)).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
        for dep in deps{for later in writes.iter().filter(|o|o.p.name!=w.p.name&&(o.table==w.table&&o.op==WriteOp::Update||bound.schema.tables[&o.table].columns.values().any(|k|matches!(k.required(),ColumnType::Ref{table} if table==&w.table)))){
            if conditions(&later.context).iter().any(|e|reads(e).contains(&dep)){continue;}
            note(out,&later.site,"N_STALE_CREATION_CHECK",format!("{}: {} creation by {} checks mutable {dep}, but this later {} write does not recheck it",later.p.name,w.table,w.p.name,later.table),"if eligibility must remain current at approval/transition, recheck it there; declare independent intent and test a create -> eligibility change -> approval history");
        }}
    }
}

fn duration_number(e:&Expr)->Option<i64>{
    let Expr::Literal{value:ast::Literal::Number(s),..}=e else{return None;};
    for (suffix,scale) in [("ms",1),("s",1000),("m",60_000),("h",3_600_000),("d",86_400_000)]{
        if let Some(n)=s.strip_suffix(suffix){return n.parse::<i64>().ok()?.checked_mul(scale);}
    }s.parse().ok()
}
fn duration_notes(writes:&[Write<'_>],out:&mut Vec<Diagnostic>){
    let mut bounds=BTreeMap::<(String,String,String),BTreeMap<i64,BTreeSet<String>>>::new();
    let mut sites=BTreeMap::new();
    for w in writes{for condition in conditions(&w.context){walk(condition,|e|{
        let Expr::Binary{op,left,right,..}=e else{return;};if !matches!(op.as_str(),">"|">="|"<"|"<="){return;}
        for (field,clock,cmp) in [(left.as_ref(),right.as_ref(),op.as_str()),(right.as_ref(),left.as_ref(),match op.as_str(){">"=>"<",">="=>"<=","<"=>">",_=>">="})]{
            let Expr::Binary{op:offset,left:now,right:amount,..}=clock else{continue;};
            if !matches!(now.as_ref(),Expr::Name{name,..} if name=="now")||!matches!(offset.as_str(),"-"|"+"){continue;}
            let Some(mut duration)=duration_number(amount)else{continue;};if offset=="-"{duration=-duration;}
            let target=match field{
                Expr::Member{base,name,..}=>reads(base).into_iter().next().map(|table|(table,name.clone())),
                Expr::Name{name,..}=>writes.iter().filter(|a|a.p.name==w.p.name).find_map(|a|a.fields.iter().find(|f|field_value(f).is_some_and(|e|matches!(e,Expr::Name{name:n,..} if n==name))).map(|f|(a.table.clone(),f.name.clone()))),_=>None,
            };
            if let Some((table,column))=target{let key=(table,column,cmp.to_string());sites.entry(key.clone()).or_insert_with(||w.site.clone());bounds.entry(key).or_default().entry(duration).or_default().insert(w.p.name.clone());}
        }
    });}}
    for ((table,column,cmp),values) in bounds{if values.len()<2{continue;}
        let entries=values.iter().map(|(ms,ops)|format!("now {ms:+}ms in {}",ops.iter().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("; ");
        note(out,&sites[&(table.clone(),column.clone(),cmp.clone())],"N_DURATION_BOUNDS",format!("{table}.{column}: inconsistent `{cmp}` duration bounds across operations: {entries}; are these different windows intentional?"),"use the specification's duration consistently at creation, renewal and approval, or document the deliberate workflow difference");
    }
}

#[test]fn directed_boolean_lifecycle_keeps_role_revocation_between_open_and_finalize(){

 let mut e=snapback4_core::Engine::memory(b.schema.clone(),b.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone())).unwrap();

 let mut c=Campaign::default();challenges::fixture_observers(&b,&e,&mut c);

}

-- Wash Fold Laundry — Snapback 4 (Quarry) schema, v1.
-- Roles: customer, attendant, driver, owner (roster in `roles`).
-- Operators bootstrap via the service:operator seat; owners manage staff after that.

use identity password

table roles:
  principal: principal
  role: enum('customer', 'attendant', 'driver', 'owner')
  unique principal
  immutable principal
  read <- .principal = viewer or exists roles[viewer, role = 'attendant'] or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner'] or viewer = 'service:operator'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:operator' or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']
  update <- viewer = 'service:operator' or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']
  delete <- viewer = 'service:operator' or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']
  online only

table driverRoutes:
  driver: principal
  route: text 1..40
  unique driver
  immutable driver
  read <- .driver = viewer or exists roles[viewer, role = 'attendant'] or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner'] or viewer = 'service:operator'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:operator' or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']
  update <- viewer = 'service:operator' or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']
  delete <- viewer = 'service:operator' or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']
  online only

-- One bag drop-off. priceCents is computed server-side from weight (25c/oz).
table orders:
  customer: principal
  weightOz: int 1..3200
  priceCents: int 0..1000000
  tagPhoto: image <=5000000
  status: enum('washing', 'drying', 'folding', 'ready', 'pickedup', 'donated') default 'washing'
  route: text 1..40
  droppedAt: time default now
  readyAt: time ?
  pickedUpAt: time ?
  immutable customer, weightOz, droppedAt
  read <- .customer = viewer or exists roles[viewer, role = 'attendant'] or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner'] or exists driverRoutes[viewer, route = .route]
  insert <- exists roles[viewer, role = 'attendant'] or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']
  update <- exists roles[viewer, role = 'attendant'] or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner'] or .customer = viewer
  online only

-- A customer's lost/damaged claim; only the owner decides it.

  order: orders
  customer: principal
  reason: enum('lost', 'damaged')
  detail: text 1..500
  status: enum('pending', 'approved', 'denied') default 'pending'
  refundCents: int ?
  at: time default now
  unique order else ALREADY_CLAIMED 'This order already has a claim.'
  immutable order, customer, reason, detail, at
  read <- .customer = viewer or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']
  insert <- .customer = viewer
  update <- exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']
  online only

query myRole():
  return roles[viewer]

query allRoles(c: cursor ?):
  require exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner'] else NOT_OWNER
  return roles first 50 by .id after c

query myOrders(c: cursor ?):
  return orders where .customer = viewer last 50 by .droppedAt after c { *, overdue: .droppedAt + 2d < now and (.status = 'washing' or .status = 'drying' or .status = 'folding') }

query receipt(order: orders):
  return orders[order]

query receipts(c: cursor ?):
  return orders where .customer = viewer and (.status = 'ready' or .status = 'pickedup') last 50 by .droppedAt after c

query board(c: cursor ?):
  require exists roles[viewer, role = 'attendant'] or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner'] else NOT_STAFF
  return orders where .status = 'washing' or .status = 'drying' or .status = 'folding' or .status = 'ready' last 100 by .droppedAt after c { *, overdue: .droppedAt + 2d < now and (.status = 'washing' or .status = 'drying' or .status = 'folding') }

  require exists roles[viewer, role = 'attendant'] or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner'] else NOT_STAFF
  return orders where .droppedAt + 2d < now and (.status = 'washing' or .status = 'drying' or .status = 'folding') last 100 by .droppedAt after c

mutation provision(principal: principal, role: enum('customer', 'attendant', 'driver', 'owner')):
  require viewer = 'service:operator' or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner'] else NOT_OPERATOR
  return upsert roles[principal] { principal, role }

mutation assignRoute(driver: principal, route: text 1..40):
  require viewer = 'service:operator' or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner'] else NOT_OWNER
  require exists roles[driver, role = 'driver'] else NOT_DRIVER
  return upsert driverRoutes[driver] { driver, route }

mutation dropOff(customer: principal, weightOz: int 1..3200, tagPhoto: image <=5000000, route: text 1..40):
  require exists roles[viewer, role = 'attendant'] or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner'] else NOT_ATTENDANT
  require exists roles[customer, role = 'customer'] else NOT_CUSTOMER 'Drop-offs are logged for customer accounts.'
  return insert orders { customer, weightOz, priceCents: weightOz * 25, tagPhoto, route }

mutation advanceStatus(order: orders, status: enum('drying', 'folding', 'ready')):
  o = orders[order] ! else NOT_FOUND
  require exists roles[viewer, role = 'attendant'] or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner'] else NOT_STAFF
  require (o.status = 'washing' and status = 'drying') or (o.status = 'drying' and status = 'folding') or (o.status = 'folding' and status = 'ready') else BAD_TRANSITION 'Bags move washing -> drying -> folding -> ready.'
  update orders[order] { status, readyAt: now if status = 'ready' else o.readyAt }
  return null

mutation pickup(order: orders):
  o = orders[order] ! else NOT_FOUND
  require o.customer = viewer or exists roles[viewer, role = 'attendant'] or exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner'] else NOT_YOURS
  require o.status = 'ready' else NOT_READY 'This bag is not ready for pickup.'
  require (o.readyAt ?? 0) > now - 30d else TOO_LATE 'Unclaimed bags are donated after 30 days.'
  update orders[order] { status: 'pickedup', pickedUpAt: now }
  return null

mutation fileClaim(order: orders, reason: enum('lost', 'damaged'), detail: text 1..500):
  o = orders[order] ! else NOT_FOUND
  require o.customer = viewer else NOT_YOURS 'Claims are filed by the order owner.'
  return insert claims { order, customer: viewer, reason, detail }

mutation decideClaim(claim: claims, approve: bool, refundCents: int ?):
  c = claims[claim] ! else NOT_FOUND
  require exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner'] else NOT_OWNER 'Only the store owner approves refunds.'
  require c.status = 'pending' else ALREADY_DECIDED 'This claim was already decided.'
  require not approve or (refundCents ?? 0) > 0 else BAD_REFUND 'An approved refund needs an amount.'
  require approve or refundCents = null else BAD_REFUND 'A denied claim carries no amount.'
  update claims[claim] { status: 'approved' if approve else 'denied', refundCents }
  return null

job donateUnclaimed(periodStart: time, periodEnd: time):
  for o in orders by .id:
    if o.status = 'ready' and (o.readyAt ?? 0) < periodEnd - 30d:
      update orders[o.id] { status: 'donated' }

/// `{ column: value }` a read pins, its keys resolved against the table's
/// indexes (named keys, or positional keys of the one unique index of that
/// width) and its `where` equalities. None when a positional key is ambiguous.
pub(super) fn pins(catalog: &Catalog, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<BTreeMap<String, Expr>> {
    let info = catalog.tables.get(&read.table)?;
    let mut out = BTreeMap::new();
    let positional: Vec<&KeyPart> = read.keys.iter().filter(|k| k.column.is_none()).collect();
    if !positional.is_empty() {
        let columns = match read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|index| &index.name == name)) {
            Some(index) => index.columns.clone(),
            None => {
                let unique: Vec<&IndexDecl> = info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns.len() == read.keys.len() && !index.columns.iter().any(|c| c == "id")).collect();
                match unique.as_slice() { [index] => index.columns.clone(), _ => return None }
            }
        };
        if columns.len() < read.keys.len() { return None; }
        for (column, key) in columns.iter().zip(&read.keys) { out.insert(key.column.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| column.clone()), key.value.clone()); }
    } else {
        for key in &read.keys { out.insert(key.column.clone()?, key.value.clone()); }
    }
    let mut filters = Vec::new();
    if let Some(filter) = &read.filter { super::intent::split((**filter).clone(), &mut filters); }
    for filter in filters {
        if let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = &filter && op == "="
            && let Some(column) = super::intent::column(left) { out.entry(column.to_owned()).or_insert_with(|| (**right).clone()); }
    }
    Some(out)
}

/// Every `exists` a condition asserts (outside `not`): `a and exists t[…]`, either side of `or`.
fn asserted_probes<'a>(condition: &'a Expr, out: &mut Vec<&'a ReadExpr>) {
    match condition {
        Expr::Exists { read, .. } => out.push(read),
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "and" || op == "or" => { asserted_probes(left, out); asserted_probes(right, out); }
        _ => {}
    }
}

fn requires(body: &[Stmt], out: &mut Vec<(Expr, String)>) {
    for statement in body {
        match statement {
            Stmt::Require { condition, code, .. } => out.push((condition.clone(), code.clone())),
            Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => { requires(then_body, out); requires(else_body, out); }
            Stmt::For { body, .. } | Stmt::As { body, .. } => requires(body, out),
            _ => {}
        }
    }

    // ----- writes -----

    /// Bind a write. When `valued`, the write yields its row (an insert's
    /// image, an update's or upsert's re-read row, null for a delete).
    fn write(&mut self, expr: &A, out: &mut setup::Body, valued: bool) -> Fallible<Option<(Expr, Ty)>> {
        let result = self.write_inner(expr, out, valued);
        self.may_have_written = true;
        self.invalidate_after_write(expr);
        result
    }

    fn write_inner(&mut self, expr: &A, out: &mut setup::Body, valued: bool) -> Fallible<Option<(Expr, Ty)>> {
        if self.viewer_depth.is_some() { return Err(job_reads::read_only(expr.site())); }
        let A::Write { op, table, keys, fields, if_absent, condition, site } = expr else { unreachable!() };
        // @ref LLP 3000.036#12 — `delete row` and `update row { … }` write the
        // row a present row variable holds: the same write as `t[row]`.
        if keys.is_empty() && matches!(op, WriteOp::Delete | WriteOp::Update) {
            let resolved = match self.lookup(table) {
                Some(Ty::Row(target)) => Some(target.clone()),
                _ => None,
            };
            let Some(target) = resolved else {
                let (message, rewrite) = match self.lookup(table) {
                    Some(Ty::Optional(inner)) if matches!(inner.as_ref(), Ty::Row(_)) => {
                        let Ty::Row(target) = inner.as_ref() else { unreachable!() };
                        (format!("{table} may be null; {} needs a present row", op.word()), format!("{table} = {target}[id] ! else NOT_FOUND, then {} {table}", op.word()))
                    }
                    Some(other) => (format!("{} {table} needs a row variable; {table} is {}", op.word(), other.name()), format!("{} t[{table}]", op.word())),
                    None if self.catalog.tables.contains_key(table.as_str()) => (format!("{} {table} names no row", op.word()), format!("{} {table}[id]{}", op.word(), if *op == WriteOp::Update { " { … }" } else { "" })),
                    None => (format!("unknown row variable {table}"), format!("bind it first: row = t[id] ! else NOT_FOUND, then {} row", op.word())),
                };
                return Err(unknown(site, message, rewrite));
            };
            let key = ast::KeyPart { column: None, value: A::Name { name: table.clone(), site: site.clone() }, site: site.clone() };
            let resolved = A::Write { op: *op, table: target, keys: vec![key], fields: fields.clone(), if_absent: *if_absent, condition: condition.clone(), site: site.clone() };
            return self.write_inner(&resolved, out, valued);
        }
        if self.in_prelude {
            return Err(placement(site, "a write belongs to a chunk", format!("move {} {table} inside a top-level `for row in table:` job sweep", op.word())));
        }
        if self.kind == ProgramKind::Query {
            return Err(Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_PLACEMENT", format!("queries cannot {} {table}", op.word()), "move the write into a mutation").guide("programs"));
        }
        let info = self.table(table, site)?;
        if info.maintained.is_some() {
            return Err(unknown(site, format!("{table} is a maintained aggregate; write its source rows instead"), format!("{} {}", op.word(), info.maintained.as_ref().map(|m| m.source.clone()).unwrap_or_default())));
        }
        for field in fields {
            if info.decl.columns.iter().any(|c| c.name == field.name && c.sequence.is_some()) {
                return Err(Diagnostic::new(&field.site, "E_SEQUENCE_SUPPLIED", format!("{table}.{} is assigned by the engine", field.name),
                    format!("omit {} from the {} payload", field.name, op.word())).guide("writes"));
            }
            if super::columns::fields(info).any(|name|name == field.name) {
                return Err(super::columns::refusal(&field.site,format!("{table}.{} is engine-maintained; write its source rows instead",field.name)));
            }
        }
        self.system_principal_write(info, op, fields, *if_absent, site)?;
        self.note_denied_write(info, op, fields, site);
        // Writes share table-level residency facts, but every source occurrence
        // (including an expanded helper) belongs in a changed-prediction note.
        let write_name = format!("{} {table}", op.word());
        self.sources.insert(format!("write/{write_name}/{site}"), (site.clone(), write_name));
        match op {
            WriteOp::Insert => {
                let (row, row_ty) = self.write_row(info, fields, None, site, out)?;
                let row_ty = if self.schema.tables[table].column_reads.is_empty() || self.ordinary_write_lifetime(table) && self.row_independent_read_proved(table) { row_ty } else { row_ty.optional() };
                let tmp = self.temp("inserted");
                out.push(Stmt::Const { name: tmp.clone(), value: row });
                if *if_absent {
                    let result = self.temp("absent");
                    out.push(Stmt::InsertIfAbsent { table: table.clone(), row: ir::var(&tmp), result: result.clone() });
                    return Ok(valued.then(|| (self.project_insert(info, ir::var(&result)), row_ty.optional())));
                }
                out.push(Stmt::Insert { table: table.clone(), row: ir::var(&tmp) });
                Ok(valued.then(|| (self.project_insert(info, ir::var(&tmp)), row_ty)))
            }
            WriteOp::Update | WriteOp::Upsert => {
                // @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — upsert's insert arm has no old row on which to decide a condition.
                if *op == WriteOp::Upsert && condition.is_some() {
                    return Err(unknown(site, "upsert cannot take a condition", "a condition belongs on update; an upsert inserts or patches"));
                }
                // @ref LLP 3000.007#8 — item 7: target resolution and conditional old images belong to the engine.
                let key = self.write_key(info, keys, site, out).map_err(|error| self.extra_write_test(info, expr, error))?;
                let upsert = *op == WriteOp::Upsert;
                if upsert && fields.iter().any(|f| f.name == "id") {
                    return Err(unknown(site, "upsert cannot spell id; the key names the row", "remove id from the patch; the key names the row"));
                }
                let (patch, _) = self.write_row(info, fields, Some("patch"), site, out)?;
                self.immutable_patch(info, &key, &patch, fields, site, out, upsert, condition.is_some())?;
                let condition = if let Some(condition) = condition {
                    let old = self.temp("condition");
                    let prior = self.focal.replace((old.clone(), Ty::Row(table.clone())));
                    let mut setup = setup::Body::default();
                    let bound = self.expr(condition, &mut setup);
                    self.focal = prior;
                    let (value, ty) = bound?;
                    if !ty.is_bool() {
                        return Err(type_error(condition.site(), format!("update's condition must be bool, got {}", ty.name()), "update t[key] { body } if .body = expected"));
                    }
                    out.nest(&format!("{}.condition", out.len()), &setup.ownership);
                    Some(ir::WriteCondition { old, setup: setup.code, value })
                } else { None };
                let result = valued.then(|| self.temp("written"));
                let target = Read { site: site.to_string(), table: table.clone(), index: key.index, key: key.key, provenance: KeyProvenance::Shape };
                out.push(Stmt::WriteTarget { target, patch, upsert, condition, result: result.clone() });
                let row_ty = self.write_result_type(info);
                let row_ty = if self.ordinary_write_lifetime(table) && self.row_independent_read_proved(table) {
                    row_ty
                } else { row_ty.optional() };
                Ok(result.map(|name| (ir::var(&name), row_ty)))
            }
            WriteOp::Delete => {
                let key = self.write_key(info, keys, site, out).map_err(|error| self.extra_write_test(info, expr, error))?;
                if key.alternate {
                    let location = self.site();
                    self.sources.insert(location.clone(), (site.clone(), format!("delete {table}")));
                    out.push(Stmt::DeleteTarget { target: Read { site: location, table: table.clone(), index: key.index, key: key.key, provenance: KeyProvenance::Shape } });
                } else {
                    out.push(Stmt::Delete { table: table.clone(), key: key.key[0].clone() });
                }
                Ok(valued.then(|| (Expr::Literal(Value::Null), Ty::Null)))
            }
        }
    }

    /// `t[row]` where `row` is a present row of `t`: the same read as `t[row.id]`.
    fn row_as_key(&self, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<ReadExpr> {
        if read.keys.len() != 1 || read.keys[0].column.is_some() || read.range.is_some() || read.filter.is_some() || read.matching.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() || read.index.is_some() || !read.order_keys.is_empty() { return None; }
        let key = &read.keys[0].value;
        if !matches!(key, A::Name { .. }) { return None; }
        let (_, ty) = self.clone().expr(key, &mut setup::Body::default()).ok()?;
        if !matches!(ty, Ty::Row(ref table) | Ty::WriteRow(ref table) if *table == read.table) { return None; }
        let mut by_id = read.clone();
        by_id.keys[0].value = A::Member { base: Box::new(key.clone()), name: "id".into(), site: key.site().clone() };
        Some(by_id)
    }

    fn write_key(&mut self, info: &'a TableInfo, keys: &[ast::KeyPart], site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<WriteKey> {
        if keys.is_empty() {
            return Err(unknown(site, format!("{} needs a key: which row?", info.decl.name), format!("{}[id]", info.decl.name)));
        }
        // A row value as the key addresses the row by its id.
        if keys.len() == 1 && keys[0].column.is_none() {
            let mut row_setup = setup::Body::default();
            let (e, t) = self.expr(&keys[0].value, &mut row_setup)?;
            if let Ty::Row(table) | Ty::WriteRow(table) = t.required()
                && table == info.decl.name
            {
                out.extend(row_setup);
                let id = self.temp("key");
                out.push(Stmt::Let { name: id.clone(), value: ir::field(e, "id") });
                return Ok(WriteKey { index: "byId".into(), key: vec![ir::var(&id)], alternate: false });
            }
        }
        let read = ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), table: info.decl.name.clone(), keys: keys.to_vec(), index: None, range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new(), site: site.clone() };
        let (bound, index, _) = self.select_index(info, &read, ReadMode::Point, out)?;
        super::point_key::check(info, &index, &read, &bound.iter().map(|(column, _, ty)|
            (column.as_deref(), !ty.is_optional() && *ty != Ty::Unknown)).collect::<Vec<_>>(), false)?;
        let key = self.order_keys(info, &index, bound)?;
        if index.name == "byId" {
            let id = self.temp("key");
            out.push(Stmt::Let { name: id.clone(), value: key[0].clone() });
            return Ok(WriteKey { index: "byId".into(), key: vec![ir::var(&id)], alternate: false });
        }
        Ok(WriteKey { index: index.name, key, alternate: true })
    }

    fn write_row(&mut self, info: &'a TableInfo, fields: &[ObjectField], current: Option<&str>, site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        objects::explicit_fields(fields.iter().filter(|f| !f.spread).map(|f| (f.name.as_str(), &f.site)))?;
        let expanded;
        let fields = if fields.iter().any(|field| field.spread) {
            expanded = self.expand_object_fields(fields, Some(info), out)?;
            &expanded
        } else { fields };
        for field in fields {
            if super::columns::fields(info).any(|name|name == field.name) {
                return Err(super::columns::refusal(&field.site,format!("{}.{} is engine-maintained; write its source rows instead",info.decl.name,field.name)));
            }
        }
        match current {
            None => {
                if fields.iter().any(|f| f.name == "id") {
                    let (given, ty) = fields.iter().find(|f| f.name == "id").map(|f| f.value.as_ref().map(|v| self.expr_as_reference(v, out, &Ty::Id)).unwrap_or_else(|| self.name("id", site))).unwrap()?;
                    if !ty.assignable_to(&Ty::Id) { return Err(type_error(site,format!("an inserted id must be an id, got {}",ty.name()),"use row.id for a row snapshot; reference columns accept a required same-table row")); }
                    return self.write_row_with_id(info, &fields.iter().filter(|f| f.name != "id").cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>(), given, site, out);
                }
                self.write_row_with_id(info, fields, Expr::Input(Input::NewId), site, out)
            }
            Some(_) => {
                // A patch: only the named columns, each typed.
                let mut exprs = Vec::<(String, Expr)>::new();
                let mut types = BTreeMap::new();
                for field in fields {
                    if field.name == "id" {
                        return Err(unknown(&field.site, "update cannot change id", "remove id from the patch"));
                    }
                    let Some(scalar) = info.columns.get(&field.name) else {
                        return Err(unknown(&field.site, format!("{} has no column {}", info.decl.name, field.name), name_rewrite(&field.name, info.columns.keys().map(String::as_str), None)));
                    };
                    let expected = Ty::from_scalar(scalar);
                    let mut setup = setup::Body::default();
                    let (e, t) = self.field_value(field, &expected, &mut setup)?;
                    literal_in_range(info, &field.name, scalar, &e, &field.site)?;

fn deserialize_text_format<'de, D: serde::Deserializer<'de>>(deserializer: D) -> Result<Option<crate::schema::TextFormat>, D::Error> {
    match String::deserialize(deserializer)?.as_str() {
        "handle" => Ok(Some(crate::schema::TextFormat::Handle)),
        "url" => Ok(Some(crate::schema::TextFormat::Url)),
        "email" => Ok(Some(crate::schema::TextFormat::Email)),
        _ => Err(serde::de::Error::custom("text format must be handle, url, or email")),
    }
}

/// Every read site (point, scan, merge lane, recursion) in a program body,
/// with the tables it reads: what a live subscription and `check --cost`
/// both need.
pub fn read_sites(program: &Program) -> Vec<ReadSite> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    stmts_read_sites(&program.body, &mut out);
    out
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
pub struct ReadSite {
    pub site: String,
    pub table: String,
    pub index: String,
    /// `point`, `scan`, or `walk`.
    pub kind: &'static str,
    pub take: u64,
}

fn stmts_read_sites(stmts: &[Stmt], out: &mut Vec<ReadSite>) {
    for stmt in stmts {
        match stmt {
            Stmt::Const { value, .. } | Stmt::Let { value, .. } | Stmt::Assign { value, .. } | Stmt::Push { value, .. } | Stmt::Return(value) => expr_read_sites(value, out),
            Stmt::Emit { payload: value, once, .. } => { expr_read_sites(value, out); if let Some(value) = once { expr_read_sites(value, out); } },
            Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body } => {
                expr_read_sites(condition, out);
                stmts_read_sites(then_body, out);
                stmts_read_sites(else_body, out);
            }
            Stmt::For { domain, body, .. } => {
                match domain {
                    LoopDomain::Page(scan) => scan_read_sites(scan, out),
                    LoopDomain::ExpressionRange { start, end_exclusive } => { expr_read_sites(start, out); expr_read_sites(end_exclusive, out); }
                    LoopDomain::DerivedArray { value, .. } => expr_read_sites(value, out),
                    LoopDomain::Recurse(recurse) => {
                        expr_read_sites(&recurse.seed, out);
                        out.push(ReadSite { site: recurse.site.clone(), table: recurse.table.clone(), index: recurse.index.clone(), kind: "walk", take: recurse.depth as u64 * recurse.fanout.max(1) as u64 });
                    }
                    LoopDomain::LiteralRange { .. } | LoopDomain::ArgList { .. } => {}
                }
                stmts_read_sites(body, out);
            }
            Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => expr_read_sites(condition, out),
            Stmt::Check { condition, current } => {
                expr_read_sites(condition, out);
                out.push(ReadSite { site: current.site.clone(), table: current.table.clone(), index: current.index.clone(), kind: "point", take: 1 });
            }
            Stmt::InsertIfAbsent { row, .. } | Stmt::Insert { row, .. } => expr_read_sites(row, out),
            Stmt::Update { key, patch, .. } => {
                expr_read_sites(key, out);
                expr_read_sites(patch, out);
            }
            Stmt::Upsert { key, row, .. } => {
                expr_read_sites(key, out);
                expr_read_sites(row, out);
            }
            Stmt::WriteTarget { target, patch, condition, .. } => {
                out.push(ReadSite { site: target.site.clone(), table: target.table.clone(), index: target.index.clone(), kind: "write target", take: 1 });
                for key in &target.key { expr_read_sites(key, out); }
                expr_read_sites(patch, out);
                if let Some(condition) = condition { stmts_read_sites(&condition.setup, out); expr_read_sites(&condition.value, out); }
            }
            Stmt::Delete { key, .. } => expr_read_sites(key, out),
            Stmt::DeleteTarget { target } => {
                out.push(ReadSite { site: target.site.clone(), table: target.table.clone(), index: target.index.clone(), kind: "write target", take: 1 });
                for key in &target.key { expr_read_sites(key, out); }
            }
            Stmt::Break | Stmt::Continue => {}
        }
    }
}

fn scan_read_sites(scan: &Scan, out: &mut Vec<ReadSite>) {
    if let Some(filter) = &scan.filter { expr_read_sites(filter, out); }
    if let Some(range) = &scan.range {
        for bound in [&range.gt, &range.gte, &range.lt, &range.lte].into_iter().flatten() { expr_read_sites(bound, out); }
    }
    for key in &scan.prefix {
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#18.1 — matching expressions can read independent sources; retain them in the execution facts.
        match key { IndexKeyExpr::Scalar(expr) | IndexKeyExpr::WordPrefix(expr) => expr_read_sites(expr, out) }
    }
